\displaystyle\leq(C\varepsilon_{0}+C(p)\|\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{1}\|_{L^{p}(I)}(1+\|\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{2}\|_{L^{p}(I)}))\bigg(\int_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+\|\langle\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\|_{L^{1}(I)}\bigg).

Step 5: estimate of the harmonic rest
h
.

We observe that the existence of
h
can be deduced by variational methods. Since
h
satisfies a homogeneous Neumann condition along
I
,
its extension to
B_{1}(0)
by even reflection along
I
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{h}(x)=\begin{cases}h(x^{1},x^{2})&\text{in }B_{1}^{+}(0),\\
h(x^{1},-x^{2})&\text{in }B_{1}^{-}(0)=B_{1}(0)\cap\mathbb{R}^{2}_{-},\end{cases}

will then satisfy

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} -\Delta\widetilde{h}&=0&&\text{in }B_{1}(0),\\
\tilde{h}&=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\Phi}&&\text{on }\partial B_{1}(0),\end{aligned}\right.

where
\widetilde{\lambda}_{\Phi}
similarly denotes the
extension of
\lambda_{\Phi}
to
B_{1}(0)
by even reflection along
I
.
From the classical estimate for harmonic function
we will then deduce

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(0)}|\nabla\widetilde{h}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq r^{2}\int_{B_{1}(0)}|\nabla\widetilde{\lambda}_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

and consequently,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq r^{2}\int_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(4.12)

By joining estimates (
4.11
)-(
4.12
)
we then deduce

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}|\nabla\mu|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq(C\varepsilon_{0}+C(p)\|\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{1}\|_{L^{p}(I)}(1+\|\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{2}\|_{L^{p}(I)}))\bigg(\int_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+\|\langle\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\|_{L^{1}(I)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+r^{2}\int_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

which then yields estimate (
4.6
).

As far as the estimate
(
4.7
) is concerned,
similarly as in lemma
4.1
,
we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{h}-(\widetilde{\lambda}_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{K})}\leq\frac{C}{\dist(\widetilde{K},\partial_{1}B(0))}\|\nabla\widetilde{\lambda}_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))},

where we denoted by
\widetilde{K}=K\cup\{(x^{1},-x^{2}):(x^{1},x^{2})\in K\}
,
and consequently that

\displaystyle\|h-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B^{+}_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\leq\frac{C}{\dist(K,\partial_{1}B(0))^{2}}\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}.

(4.13)

We may then write

\displaystyle\inf_{c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\|\lambda_{\Phi}-c^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\leq\|h-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\inf_{c\in\mathbb{R}}\|\mu-c\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1}^{+}(0))},

and with estimates (
4.10
)-(
4.13
)
we deduce the validity of (
4.7
).
This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.3

(Affine approximation)
.

For every
\delta>0

there exists an
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that,
for every conformal immersion
\Phi:{B_{1}(0)}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form
satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}<\varepsilon_{0},

there exists a conformal affine immersion

L=\vec{L}_{0}+x^{1}\vec{X}_{1}+x^{2}\vec{X}_{2}=\vec{L}_{0}+\langle x,\vec{X}\rangle

so that

\displaystyle\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}<\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}({B_{1}(0)})}

(4.14)

and, if
\mathrm{e}^{\nu}=|\vec{X}_{1}|=|\vec{X}_{2}|
denotes the conformal
factor of
L
,

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}<\delta.

(4.15)

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

We argue by contradiction and suppose that there exists
a
\delta>0
such that, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a conformal
Lipschitz immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form

\Phi_{k}:{B_{1}(0)}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that (writing as usual
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi_{k}}}=\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{k}}
),

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{k}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi_{k}}}\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi_{k}}=\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda_{k}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{k}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{1}{k},

(4.16)

and for every conformal affine immersion
L
there holds

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{k}-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}>\delta\|\nabla\Phi_{k}\|_{L^{2}({B_{1}(0)})}.

(4.17)

or, if
\mathrm{e}^{\nu}
denotes the conformal factor of
L
,

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{k}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}\geq\delta.

(4.18)

Since (
4.16
) is invariant under
translations and dilations in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, writing for short

c_{k}=-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{1}(0)}\lambda_{k}\,\mathrm{d}x

if we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}(x)=\mathrm{e}^{c_{k}}(\Phi_{k}(x)-\Phi_{k}(0)),\quad x\in{B_{1}(0)},

(4.19)

then
\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}:{B_{1}(0)}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
defines for every
k
a conformal
Lipschitz immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form
such that, if
\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}
denotes its conformal factor,
there holds

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{k}+c_{k},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}(0)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{1}(0)}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=0,

and

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}(0)}\mathrm{e}^{-2\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{1}{k}.

(4.20)

From the identites (
1.11
), Lemma
4.1

and
(
4.20
) it follows

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}-(\widetilde{\lambda}_{k})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle=\|\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}\|_{L^{2}({B_{1}(0)})}+C\|\nabla\vec{\widetilde{n}}_{k}\|_{L^{2}({B_{1}(0)})}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{k}\to 0\quad\text{as }k\to\infty,

so

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}\to 0\quad\text{uniformly in }B_{1/2}(0),

(4.21)

and consequently we infer that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{1/2}(0)}2\mathrm{e}^{2\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}x\to\pi/2\quad\text{as }k\to\infty,

(4.22)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{C/k}\int_{B_{1/2}(0)}\mathrm{e}^{-2\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{C}{k}\to 0\quad\text{as }k\to\infty,

(4.23)

from which we deduce, with Poincaré’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{k}\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}=\|\Phi_{k}-\Phi_{k}(0)\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}\leq C.

We deduce that, up to extraction of subsequences,
for a map
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\infty}\in W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\widetilde{\Phi}_{\infty}\quad\text{in }W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m}),

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}

\displaystyle\to 0\quad\text{in }L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{\Phi}_{\infty}\quad\text{in }W^{1,p}(B_{1/2}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})\text{ for every }1\leq p<\infty\text{ and a.e. in }B_{1/2}(0).

consequently,
\Phi_{\infty}:B_{1/2}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
is a conformal map
and, from the uniform convergence of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}

above, up to a further subsequence, its conformal factor is 1
(that is,
\tilde{\Phi}_{\infty}
is a isometric linear immersion).
Being
\nabla^{2}\Phi_{\infty}=0
,
there actually holds

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\to\widetilde{\Phi}_{\infty}\quad\text{in }W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m}).

(4.24)

Note now that from the definition (
4.19
), (
4.17
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{e}^{-c_{k}}\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}+\Phi_{k}(0)-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}\geq\delta\|\nabla\Phi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0))},

hence

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}+\mathrm{e}^{c_{k}}(\Phi_{k}(0)-L)\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}\geq\delta\|\nabla(\mathrm{e}^{c_{k}}\Phi_{k})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0))}=\delta\|\nabla(\widetilde{\Phi}_{k})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0))},

Since
L
is arbitrary,
we may consider the sequence
L_{k}=\Phi_{k}(0)+\mathrm{e}^{-c_{k}}\widetilde{\Phi}_{\infty}

and deduce from (
4.22
) that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}-\widetilde{\Phi}_{\infty}\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle\geq\delta\|\nabla(\widetilde{\Phi}_{k})\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle=\delta(\pi/2+o(1))\quad\text{as }k\to\infty,

which is a contradiction with (
4.24
).
Similarly, if (
4.18
) holds,
since the conformal factor of
L_{k}
is
\mathrm{e}^{-c_{k}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{k}-\nu_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}=\|\lambda_{k}+c_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}=\|\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}\geq\delta\quad\text{for every }k\in\mathbb{N},

which contradicts (
4.21
).
∎

4.2
Construction of Suitable Competitors

Lemma 4.4

(Interior Competitors)
.

Let
\Phi:{B_{1}(0)}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
be a
conformal immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded
second fundamental form.
For every
\delta>0
there
there exists an
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that if

\displaystyle\int_{B_{4r}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}<\varepsilon_{0},

for some
0<r\leq 1/4
,
then there exists a
\rho\in[r/2,r]

such that the solution to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta^{2}\psi&=0&&\text{in }B_{\rho}(0),\\
\psi&=\Phi&&\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0),\\
\nabla\psi&=\nabla\Phi&&\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0),\end{aligned}\right.

(4.25)

defines an immersion which satisfies:

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}_{g_{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}\leq C\big(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\big)\int_{B_{r}(0)\setminus B_{r/2}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi},

(4.26)

and

\displaystyle|\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}(0)})-\Area(\psi)|\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}^{2},

(4.27)

and

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\|D\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}(0)})-D\Area(\psi)\|\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{r}(0))},

(4.28)

where
C>0
independent of
r
and
\Phi
,

C_{0}>0
depends only on

\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}
and

o(\delta)/\delta\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
.

Remark 4.5
.

We note that:

(i)

For every
\rho
, the existence and uniqueness of a solution to
(
4.25
) in
W^{2,2}
is given, for example, by the fact that
such problem is the Euler-Lagrange equation for the biharmonic energy functional

\mathcal{B}(\sigma)=\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}|\Delta\sigma|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}^{2}

(or, equivalently, of the Hessian energy
\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\sigma|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}^{2}
),
subject to the prescribed boundary data.
Since
\Phi
is of class
W^{2,2}
, existence and uniqueness by an
argument similar to the one for the Dirichlet problem.

(ii)

An elementary fact that will be used in the proof of lemma
4.4
is the following.
For a function
f\in L^{1}(B_{R}(0))
(
R>0
is arbitrary) and a constant
C>0

we say that
\rho\in[R/2,R]
defines a

C
-good slice for
f
(in
B_{R}(0)\setminus B_{R/2}(0))

if
f|_{\partial B_{\rho}(0)}
is in
L^{1}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))

and there holds

\displaystyle\rho\int_{\partial B_{\rho}(0)}|f(r,\theta)|\,d\sigma\leq C\int_{B_{R}(0)\setminus B_{R/2}(0)}|f|\,\mathrm{d}x.

The existence of
C
-good slices for some
C

and any
f

is a consequence of Fubini’s theorem.
Moreover, one can check that,
for every
0<\delta<R/2
, there exists a
C_{\delta}>0

so that, for every
f\in L^{1}(B_{R}(0))
, the radii
\rho\in[R/2,R]

defining
C_{\delta}
- good slices for
f
have
Lebesgue measure at least
R/2-\delta
.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

It is sufficient to prove the thesis only for any

\delta>0
sufficiently small.
We first treat the case
r=1/4
and
argue through rescalings at the end.
In what follows, we denote by
C
a positive constant
(possibly varying line to line) which is independent of
\Phi
,
and with
C_{0}
a positive constant depending only on

\|\nabla\lambda_{1}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}
.

Step 1.

For
\varepsilon_{0}
sufficiently small as in lemma
4.1
,
we have that

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}\leq C\left(\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}+\varepsilon_{0}\right)=C_{0},

(4.29)

where
(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}
denotes the average of
\lambda_{\Phi}
over
B_{1}(0)
.
Also, for every
\delta>0
, if
\varepsilon_{0}
is sufficiently small as in lemma
4.3
,
then there exists a conformal affine immersion

L

whose conformal factor we denote by

\mathrm{e}^{\nu}
,
satisfying the following estimate

\displaystyle\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle<\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}({B_{1/2}(0)})},

(4.30)

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/4}(0))}.

\displaystyle<\delta.

(4.31)

By combining (
4.29
)-(
4.31
),
we deduce

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}+|(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}-\nu|

(4.32)

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}+\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}+\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+\delta.

It follows that

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}(1-\delta-o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(x)}\leq C_{0}(1+\delta+o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\quad\text{for }x\in B_{3/4}(0),

(4.33)

and in particular

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}(1-\delta-o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\leq\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{3/4}(0))}^{2}\leq C_{0}(1+\delta+o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}.

(4.34)

We then consider a good-slice choice
\rho\in[1/8,1/4]
so that

\Phi
and
\Phi-L
belong to
W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})
with

\displaystyle\|\Phi\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))},

\displaystyle\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))},

hence we consider the solution to (
4.25
)
for such choice of
\rho
.
Elliptic regularity theory
(see for instance
[
46
, Remark 7.2, Chapter 2]
)
implies that

\displaystyle\|\psi-L\|_{W^{5/2,2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))},

while Sobolev embedding
W^{5/2,2}\hookrightarrow C^{1,\alpha}
implies that, for every
0<\alpha<1/2
,

\displaystyle\|\psi-L\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\leq C\|\psi-L\|_{W^{5/2,2}(B_{\rho}(0))}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi-\nabla L\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\psi-L\|_{W^{5/2,2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

(Sobolev embedding)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

(elliptic estimates)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))}

(good-slice choice)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0))}

(by (
4.30
))

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta))

\displaystyle\text{(by \eqref{eq:poinwise-estimate-confact-3/4} and \eqref{eq:estimate-area-3/4})}.

Hence for
i=1,2
, we deduce the pointwise estimates in
B_{\rho}(0)

\displaystyle||\partial_{i}\psi|^{2}-\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}|

\displaystyle=||\partial_{i}\psi|-\mathrm{e}^{\nu}|\,||\partial_{i}\psi|+\mathrm{e}^{\nu}|

\displaystyle\leq|\partial_{i}\psi-\partial_{i}L|\,||\partial_{i}\psi|+\mathrm{e}^{\nu}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

and similarly

\displaystyle|\langle\partial_{1}\psi,\partial_{2}\psi\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle\partial_{1}\psi,\partial_{2}\psi\rangle-\langle\partial_{1}L,\partial_{2}L\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle(\partial_{1}\psi-\partial_{1}L),\partial_{2}\psi\rangle+\langle\partial_{1}L,(\partial_{2}\psi-\partial_{2}L)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq||\partial_{2}\psi|+|\partial_{1}L||\big(|\partial_{1}\psi-\partial_{1}L|+|\partial_{2}\psi-\partial_{2}L|\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

This implies that, if
g_{\psi}=(\langle\partial_{i}\psi,\partial_{j}\psi\rangle)_{ij}

denotes the metric associated with
\psi
,
for every vector
X=(X^{1},X^{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\left(1-C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right)|X|^{2}\leq g_{\psi}(X,X)\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\left(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right)|X|^{2},

(4.35)

where
|X|=\sqrt{(X^{1})^{2}+(X^{2})^{2}}
is the Euclidean norm of
X
.
As a consequence, we deduce that
for
\delta>0
small enough,
\psi
defines an immersion,
and in such case we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\left(1-C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right)\leq\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\left(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right),

(4.36)

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-4\nu}\left(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right)^{-2}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}\leq|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}_{g_{\psi}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{-4\nu}\left(1-C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right)^{-2}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}

consequently wee that, point-wise in
B_{\rho}(0)
,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}_{g_{\psi}}\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}\leq\frac{1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))}{\left(1-C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\right)^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-2\nu}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}.

(4.37)

Step 2: estimate for the curvature energy.

Since
\psi
solves (
4.25
),
from elliptic regularity theory, we have that
for any affine function
M(x)=\vec{M}_{0}+\langle\vec{Y},x\rangle

there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla^{2}(\psi-M)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\Phi-M\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}+\|\nabla(\Phi-M)\|_{W^{1,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\Phi-M\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}+\|\nabla(\Phi-M)\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}+\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}\right),

and hence if we suitably choose
M
so that

\displaystyle\|\nabla(\Phi-M)\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))},

\displaystyle\|\Phi-M\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla(\Phi-M)\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

(if
M(x)=\vec{M}_{0}+\langle Y,x\rangle
, it is sufficient to choose

Y=(\nabla\Phi)_{\partial B_{\rho}(0)}
and
M_{0}=(\Phi-M)_{\partial B_{\rho}(0)}
),
we actually deduce that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))},

and so, from the choice of
\rho
we made,
we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))}.

Note also that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0)}\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda_{\Phi}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.38)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\delta}\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\int_{B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0)}\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda_{\Phi}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}(1+2(\delta+o(\delta)))\int_{B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0)}\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda_{\Phi}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

By joining estimates
(
4.37
)
–
(
4.38
),
we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\psi|_{g_{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{g_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta)))\int_{B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}.

Step 3: estimate on the area.

From (
4.31
)
and
(
4.36
)
6
6

6

(
4.31
) implies

\displaystyle(1-2(\delta+o(\delta)))\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}\leq(1+2(\delta+o(\delta)))\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\quad\text{in }B_{1/4}(0).

we deduce that
(recall that
\rho\in[1/8,1/4]
),
we have

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\quad\text{in }B_{\rho}(0),

hence by integrating over
B_{\rho}(0)
we deduce

\displaystyle|\Area(\psi)-\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}(0)})|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}\left(\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}\right)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}\left|\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}\right|\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}^{2}(\delta+o(\delta)).

Step 4: estimate on the derivative of the area.

For
w\in W^{1,\infty}(B_{\rho}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})
with
compact support in
B_{\rho}(0)
,
we have

\displaystyle D\Area(\Phi)w

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}\langle\nabla\Phi,\nabla w\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle D\Area(\psi)w

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}g_{\psi}(\nabla\psi,\nabla w)\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}=\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}g_{\psi}^{ij}\langle\partial_{i}\psi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}x,

and from (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\delta^{ij}(1-C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta)))\leq g^{ij}_{\psi}\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{ij}(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))).

(4.39)

We also observe that

\|\nabla\psi-\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}\leq C\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0))}.

(4.40)

Moreover

\displaystyle g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\psi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-\delta^{ij}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle

\displaystyle=g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\psi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}

\displaystyle+g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-\delta^{ij}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle.

By using (
4.39
) we get that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}\left|g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-\delta^{ij}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\right|\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}|\langle\nabla\Phi,\nabla w\rangle|\mathrm{d}x

(4.41)

and

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}\left|g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\psi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}-g^{ij}_{\psi}\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{j}w\rangle\sqrt{\det g_{\psi}}\right|\mathrm{d}x

(4.42)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\nabla\psi-\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}({B_{\rho}(0)})}\leq C_{0}\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0))}.

By combining estimates (
4.41
) and (
4.42
) we get

\displaystyle|D\Area(\psi)w-D\Area(\Phi)w|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\int_{B_{\rho}(0)}|\langle\partial_{i}\Phi,\partial_{i}w\rangle|\mathrm{d}x+C_{0}\|\nabla\psi-\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}({B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0))}

Step 5: the estimates for a general
r
.

If
0<r<1/4
,
we may reduce to the case
r=1/4
:
indeed, if
we consider the rescaling
\widetilde{\Phi}(x)=\Phi(4rx)

for
x\in{B_{1}(0)}
, by conformal invariance we
have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{4r}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle=\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi},

and the area functional, the Dirichlet energy,
solution to the problem (
4.25
)
and the
L^{(2,\infty)}
-seminorm of
\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}

are invariant by rescalings as well

7
7

7

to see this last fact,
note that, if
d_{\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}}(t)=\ \mathcal{L}^{2}\left(\{x:|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}(x)|>t\}\right\})

denotes the distribution function of
\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}
, and
\sigma>0
,

{\lambda_{\Phi}}_{\Phi,\sigma}(x)=\lambda_{\Phi}(\sigma x)

has distribution function

d_{\nabla\lambda_{\Phi,\sigma}}(t)=\sigma^{-2}d_{\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}}(t/\sigma)
.
Consequently

\displaystyle\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi,\sigma}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}}=\sup_{t>0}t\sqrt{d_{\nabla\lambda_{\Phi,\sigma}}(t)}=\sup_{u>0}u\sqrt{d_{\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}}(u)}=\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}}.

.
We may apply the previous steps to
\widetilde{\Phi}
,
estimate
\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{4r}(0))}

with
\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}

and then rescale back.
∎

Definition 4.6
.

An immersion
\Phi:B_{1}^{+}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

is said to have
flat geometric boundary data

on the base diameter
I
if
there holds

\displaystyle\Phi(x^{1},0)\in\spanop_{\mathbb{R}}\{\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\vec{n}_{\Phi}(x^{1},0)=\vec{\varepsilon}_{3}\wedge\ldots\wedge\vec{\varepsilon}_{m-2}\quad\text{for }(x^{1},0)\in I.

For a conformal immersion
\Phi:B_{1}^{+}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
with flat geometric boundary data on
I
,
its
geometric reflection
along
I,

\widehat{\Phi}:B_{1}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
is defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}(x^{1},x^{2})=\begin{cases}\Phi(x^{1},x^{2})&\text{if }x^{2}\geq 0,\\
\Phi^{1}(x^{1},-x^{2})\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}-\sum_{l=2}^{m}\Phi^{l}(x^{1},-x^{2})\vec{\varepsilon}_{l}&\text{if }x^{2}<0.\\
\end{cases}

Note that
if
\Phi
as in definition
4.6
is conformal and with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{1}\Phi(x^{1},0)=\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(x^{1},0)}\vec{\varepsilon}_{1},\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{2}\Phi(x^{1},0)=\mathrm{e}^{\lambda(x^{1},0)}\vec{\varepsilon}_{2}.

Hence, provided that

\|\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}<+\infty
,
the geometric nature of the reflection and conformality imply that

\widehat{\Phi}
defines a conformal immersion of class

(W^{1,\infty}\cap W^{2,2})(B_{1}(0),\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
hence a Lipschitz immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form with

\displaystyle|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}(x^{1},x^{2})|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\nabla\Phi(x^{1},x^{2})|^{2}\chi_{\{x^{2}\geq 0\}}+|\nabla\Phi(x^{1},-x^{2})|^{2}\chi_{\{x^{2}<0\}},

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\widehat{\Phi}(x^{1},x^{2})|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\nabla^{2}\Phi(x^{1},x^{2})|^{2}\chi_{\{x^{2}\geq 0\}}+|\nabla^{2}\Phi(x^{1},-x^{2})|^{2}\chi_{\{x^{2}<0\}},

\displaystyle|\Delta\widehat{\Phi}(x^{1},x^{2})|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\Delta\Phi(x^{1},x^{2})|^{2}\chi_{\{x^{2}\geq 0\}}+|\Delta\Phi(x^{1},-x^{2})|^{2}\chi_{\{x^{2}<0\}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}(x^{1},x^{2})}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(x^{1},x^{2})}\chi_{\{x^{2}\geq 0\}}+\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(x^{1},-x^{2})}\chi_{\{x^{2}<0\}}.

The following is a boundary analogue of lemma
4.4
,
where additionally we have flat geometric boundary data on
I
in the sense of definition

4.6
.

Lemma 4.7
.

There exists an
\varepsilon_{0}
with the following property.
Let
\Phi:{B_{1}^{+}(0)}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
be a conformal immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded
second fundamental form and flat geometric boundary data on
I
such
that
\|\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}<+\infty
.
For every
\delta>0
there
there exists an
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that,
if

\displaystyle\int_{B_{4r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}<\varepsilon_{0},

for
0<r\leq 1/4
,
then there exists a
\rho\in[r/2,r]

and an immersion
\psi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}^{+}(0)},\mathbb{R}^{m})

which satisfies

\displaystyle\psi=\Phi\quad\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0)\cap B_{1}^{+}(0),

\displaystyle\nabla\psi=\nabla\Phi\quad\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0)\cap B_{1}^{+}(0),

has flat geometric boundary data on
\rho I

and satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{B^{+}_{\rho}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}_{g_{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\big)\int_{B^{+}_{r}(0)\setminus B^{+}_{r/2}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

(4.43)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}^{+}(0))}),

and

\displaystyle|\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}^{+}(0)})-\Area(\psi)|\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}^{+}(0))}),

(4.44)

and

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\|D\Area(\Phi|_{B^{+}_{\rho}(0)})-D\Area(\psi)\|\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{B_{r}^{+}(0))}),

(4.45)

where
C>0
is independent of
r
and
\Phi
,

C_{0}>0
may depend on

\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}
and

o(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
.

Parts of the proof of this lemma are similar to the proof
of lemma
4.4
, so we will focus on the differences.
The overall idea is first to reflect geometrically
\Phi
as in definition

4.6
, then consider the biharmonic competitor
as in lemma
4.4
and finally to smoothly “correct” it so that
it has flat geometric boundary data on
\rho I
.
Such “correction” will be essentially constructed
by means of the 1st order Taylor polynomials of such biharmonic comparison
at the points
(\rho,0)
and
(-\rho,0)
respectively.

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

As in the case of lemma
4.4
,
it sufficient to prove the thesis only for any

\delta>0
sufficiently small.
We first treat the case
r=1/4
and
argue through rescalings at the end.
In what follows, we denote by
C
a positive constant
which is independent of
\Phi
,
and with
C_{0}
a positive constant depending only on

\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}
.

Step 1.

We consider the geometric reflection of
\Phi
,
\widehat{\Phi}:B_{1}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
according to definition
4.6
.
For
\varepsilon_{0}
sufficiently small as in lemma
4.1
,
we have that

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-(\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\nabla\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}+\varepsilon_{0}\right)

(4.46)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}+\varepsilon_{0}\right)\leq C_{0},

where
(\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}})_{B_{1}(0)}
denotes the average of
\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}
over
B_{1}(0)
.
Also, for every
\delta>0
, if
\varepsilon_{0}
is sufficiently small as in lemma
4.3
,
then there exists a conformal affine immersion
L
whose conformal factor

\mathrm{e}^{\nu}
is so that the estimates

\displaystyle\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B^{+}_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle<\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}({B^{+}_{1/2}(0)})}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Phi}-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle<\delta\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}\|_{L^{2}({B_{1/2}(0)})}

(4.47)

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/4}^{+}(0))}

\displaystyle<\delta

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/4}(0))}

\displaystyle<\delta

(4.48)

are satisfied.
By combining (
4.46
)
-(
4.48
),
we deduce

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-(\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}+|(\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}})_{B_{1}(0)}-\nu|

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-(\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4}(0))}+\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-(\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}})_{B_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}+\|\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1/2}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+\delta,

consequently we point-wise estimate from above and below

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}(1-\delta-o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\widehat{\Phi}}(x)}\leq C_{0}(1+\delta+o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\quad\text{for }x\in B_{3/4}(0).

(4.49)

We then consider a good-slice choice
\rho\in[1/8,1/4]
so that

\widehat{\Phi}
and
\widehat{\Phi}-L
belong to
W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})

(equivalently, so that

\Phi
and
\Phi-L
belong to
W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0)\cap B_{1}^{+}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})
)
with

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Phi}\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\Phi}\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))},

(4.50)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Phi}-L\|_{W^{2,2}(\partial B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widehat{\Phi}-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))},

(4.51)

hence we consider the solution
for such choice of
\rho
to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta^{2}\psi_{0}&=0&&\text{in }B_{\rho}(0),\\
\psi_{0}&=\widehat{\Phi}&&\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0),\\
\nabla\psi_{0}&=\nabla\widehat{\Phi}&&\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0),\end{aligned}\right.

which satisfies, as in lemma
4.4
, the estimates

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}-L\|_{W^{5/2,2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

(4.52)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{\Phi}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))}\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))},

(4.53)

and consequently, by Sobolev embedding
W^{5/2,2}\hookrightarrow C^{1,\alpha}

for every
0<\alpha<1/2
we have,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

(4.54)

The estimates (
4.52
) and (
4.54
) will be crucial for what follows.
If
T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}(x)
and
T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(\rho,0)}(x)

denote the Taylor polynomial of
\psi_{0}
at the points
(-\rho,0)

(\rho,0)
respectively,
we may write, for
x\in B_{\rho}(0)
,

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}(x)

\displaystyle=T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x):=\Phi(-\rho,0)+\left\langle\nabla\Phi(-\rho,0),\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(\rho,0)}(x)

\displaystyle=T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x):=\Phi(\rho,0)+\left\langle\nabla\Phi(\rho,0),\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle,

where the expressions on the right-hand sides have a well-defined meaning
because of our good-slice choice of
\rho
.
Note moreover that
T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}(x)
and
T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(\rho,0)}(x)

are conformal, they define a parametrization of the plane
\spanop\{\vec{\varepsilon}_{1},\vec{\varepsilon}_{2}\}

and are so that
T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}(x^{1},0),T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(\rho,0)}(x^{1},0)\in\spanop\{\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}\}
(in particular they have flat geometric boundary data
on
\rho I
according
to definition
4.6
).
For every
x\in B_{\rho}(0)
,
by virtue of Taylor’s theorem there exists
\xi\in((-\rho,0),x)\subset B_{\rho}(0)

so that

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(x)-T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}(x)=\left\langle(\nabla\psi_{0}(\xi)-\nabla\psi_{0}(-\rho,0)),\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle,

consequently we deduce
that for every
x\in B_{\rho}(0)
there holds

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}(x)-T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha},

(4.55)

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi_{0}(x)-\nabla T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(-\rho,0)}|

\displaystyle\leq[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\alpha},

(4.56)

and similarly that

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}(x)-T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(\rho,0)}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha},

(4.57)

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi_{0}(x)-\nabla T^{1}_{\psi_{0},(\rho,0)}|

\displaystyle\leq[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\alpha}.

(4.58)

Note also that for every
x\in B_{\rho}(0)
we may estimate

\displaystyle|T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x)-L(x)|

\displaystyle=\left|T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x)-L(-\rho,0)-\left\langle\nabla L(-\rho,0),\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\Phi(-\rho,0)-L(-\rho,0)|+|\nabla\Phi(-\rho,0)-\nabla L(-\rho,0)|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|,

\displaystyle|\nabla T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}-\nabla L|

\displaystyle=|\nabla\Phi(-\rho,0)-\nabla L(-\rho,0)|,

and similarly

\displaystyle|T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-L(x)|

\displaystyle\leq|\Phi(\rho,0)-L(\rho,0)|+|\nabla\Phi(\rho,0)-\nabla L(\rho,0)|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|,

\displaystyle|\nabla T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}-\nabla L(\rho,0)|

\displaystyle=|\nabla\Phi(\rho,0)-\nabla L(\rho,0)|,

hence thanks to (
4.47
)-(
4.51
),

\displaystyle\|T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}-L\|_{C^{1}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

(4.59)

\displaystyle\|T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}-L\|_{C^{1}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

(4.60)

Consequently, with (
4.54
) we deduce

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}-T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\psi_{0}-L\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}+\|T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}-L\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

and similarly

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}-T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

Step 2.

We now let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a non-negative smooth function so that

\displaystyle f(t)=\begin{cases}0&\text{for }t\leq-\rho/2,\\
1&\text{for }t\geq\rho/2,\end{cases}

and we set, for
x\in B_{\rho}(0)
,

\displaystyle\phi(x)=T_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}^{1}(x)+f(x^{1})(T_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x)).

Such function has range in the plane
\spanop\{\vec{\varepsilon}_{1},\vec{\varepsilon}_{2}\}

and is so that
\phi(x^{1},0)\in\spanop\{\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}\}
.
Moreover, since

\displaystyle\partial_{1}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{1}T_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}^{1}(x)+f^{\prime}(x^{1})(T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x))

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+f(x^{1})(\partial_{1}T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-\partial_{1}T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)}\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}+f^{\prime}(x^{1})(T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x))

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+f(x^{1})(\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\rho,0)}-\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)})\vec{\varepsilon}_{1},

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)}+f(x^{1})(\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\rho,0)}-\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)})\big)\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+f^{\prime}(x^{1})(T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x))

\displaystyle\partial_{2}\phi(x)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)}+f(x^{1})(\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\rho,0)}-\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)})\big)\vec{\varepsilon}_{2},

we have that, if
\delta>0
is chosen small enough, it defines an immersion.
Indeed, on the one hand from
(
4.59
)-(
4.60
)
we can estimate

\displaystyle\|T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}-T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq\|T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}-L\|_{C^{0}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}+\|T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}-L\|_{C^{0}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

on the other hand, from (
4.48
)
and the mean value theorem we may estimate

\displaystyle|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)}-\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\rho,0)}|

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)-\lambda_{\Phi}(\rho,0)|\sup\{\mathrm{e}^{\xi}:\xi\in[\lambda_{\Phi}(\pm\rho,0),\lambda_{\Phi}(\mp\rho]\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\sup\{\mathrm{e}^{\xi}:\xi\in[\nu-\delta,\nu+\delta]\}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\mathrm{e}^{\nu},

hence
we have the estimates, uniformly in
x\in B_{\rho}(0)
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{1}\phi(x)|

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(-\rho,0)}-C\delta\mathrm{e}^{\nu}-C_{0}\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}((-\rho,\rho))}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta))

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\delta}-C\delta-C_{0}\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}((-\rho,\rho))}(\delta+o(\delta))\big),

and similarly

\displaystyle|\partial_{2}\phi(x)|

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\delta}-C\delta\big),

and

\displaystyle|\langle\partial_{1}(x),\partial_{2}\phi(x)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}\big(\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(-\rho,\rho)}C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\big)(\mathrm{e}^{\delta}+C\delta).

These inequalities imply the immersive nature of
\phi
if

\delta>0
is chosen small enough.
Note also that thanks to (
4.59
)-(
4.60
)
there holds

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\phi(x)|

\displaystyle\leq|f^{\prime\prime}(x^{1})||T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x)|+2|f^{\prime}(x^{1})||\nabla T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}-\nabla T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

Since we may write

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(x)-\phi(x)=(1-f(x^{1}))(T^{1}_{\Phi,(-\rho,0)}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))+f(x^{1})(T^{1}_{\Phi,(\rho,0)}(x)-\psi_{0}(x)),

we deduce
thanks to
(
4.55
)
–
(
4.56
)
–
(
4.57
)
–
(
4.58
)
that

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}(x)-\phi(x)|

\displaystyle\leq[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\bigg((1-f(x^{1}))\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+f(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}\bigg),

(4.61)

\displaystyle|\nabla\psi_{0}(x)-\nabla\phi(x)|

\displaystyle\leq[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\bigg(|f^{\prime}(x^{1})|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+(1-f(x^{1}))\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\alpha}

(4.62)

\displaystyle\phantom{[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\bigg(|f^{\prime}(x^{1})|\bigg(}+|f^{\prime}(x^{1})|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+f(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\alpha}\bigg),

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}(x)-\nabla^{2}\phi(x)|

\displaystyle\leq|\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}(x)|+C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

(4.63)

Step 3.

In this step we construct a function
\chi:B_{\rho}(0)\to\mathbb{R}
,
which we will use in a moment, with the following properties:
it is supported in
B_{\rho}(0)\setminus\{(-\rho,0),(\rho,0)\}
,
it is
smooth away from
(-\rho,0),(\rho,0)
and is so that

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle\equiv 1\text{ in a neighbourhood of }(-\rho,\rho)\times\{0\}\text{ in }B_{\rho}(0)\text{ which shrinks at }(\pm\rho,0),

\displaystyle|\nabla\chi(x)|

\displaystyle\sim\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-1}\text{ as }x\to(-\rho,0),\quad|\nabla\chi(x)|\sim\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-1}\text{ as }x\to(\rho,0),

(4.64)

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\chi(x)|

\displaystyle\sim\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-2}\text{ as }x\to(-\rho,0),\quad|\nabla^{2}\chi(x)|\sim\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-2}\text{ as }x\to(\rho,0).

(4.65)

Such function may be constructed as follows.
Let
k_{0}:S^{1}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function
so that, for an angle
\beta
to be specified below, it satisfies

\displaystyle k_{0}(\theta)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }-\beta\leq\theta\leq\beta,\\
0&\text{if }\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]\setminus[-\beta,\beta].\end{cases}

\displaystyle\chi_{0}(x)=r\,k_{0}\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}.

\beta
\scriptstyle+
-\rho
\scriptstyle+
\rho
\scriptstyle+
-\frac{\rho}{2}
\scriptstyle+
\frac{\rho}{2}
\scriptstyle+
\frac{\rho}{4}
\scriptstyle+
-\frac{\rho}{4}
\scriptstyle+
\frac{\rho}{2}
\scriptstyle+
-\frac{\rho}{2}
r
x^{1}
x^{2}

Figure 3:
definition of
\alpha
and
r
in
the construction of
\chi

We extend it by homogeneity to
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
,
we choose
\beta=\arccos(2/\sqrt{5})
and we rescale it of a factor
r=\rho\sqrt{5}/4

so to match the construction indicated in Fig.

3
:

Such function will satisfy

\displaystyle|\nabla\chi_{0}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\chi_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|\left|\nabla\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C}{|x|},

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\chi_{0}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\chi_{0}^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|\left|\nabla\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|^{2}+\left|\chi_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|\left|\nabla^{2}\left(\frac{x}{|x|}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C}{|x|^{2}}.

We then let
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a triple of smooth functions
so that
f_{1}+f_{2}+f_{3}\equiv 1
and

\displaystyle f_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }t\leq-3\rho/4,\\
0&\text{if }t\geq-\rho/2,\end{cases}

\displaystyle f_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }-\rho/2\leq t\leq\rho/2\\
0&\text{if }t\geq 3\rho/4,\end{cases}

\displaystyle f_{3}(t)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }t\leq 3\rho/4,\\
1&\text{if }t\geq\rho,\end{cases}

and let
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function so that

\displaystyle\eta(x^{1},x^{2})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }x^{2}\in[-\rho/4,\rho/4],\\
0&\text{if }x^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus[-\rho/2,\rho/2].\end{cases}

The required function
\chi
is then given by

\displaystyle\chi(x)=f_{1}(x^{1})\chi_{0}(x+(\rho,0))+f_{2}(x^{1})\eta(x)+f_{3}(x^{1})\chi_{0}(x-(\rho,0)),\quad x\in B_{\rho}(0).

Step 4.

We claim that the function
\psi
in the statement of the lemma is
the given by (the restriction to
B_{1}^{+}(0)
of)
the interpolation between
\psi
and
\psi_{0}

through
\chi
, namely

\displaystyle\psi(x)=\chi(x)\phi(x)+(1-\chi(x))\psi_{0}(x),\quad x\in B_{\rho}(0).

Indeed, by construction we have that
\psi

has flat boundary data on
\rho I
according to definition
4.6

and that

\displaystyle\psi=\hat{\Phi},\quad\nabla\psi=\nabla\hat{\Phi}\quad\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0).

As the proof of lemma
4.4
shows, to prove
all the estimates in the statement, we have to verify that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi-\nabla L\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

(4.66)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla^{2}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1/4}(0)\setminus B_{1/8}(0))}+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}.

(4.67)

We may write

\displaystyle\psi(x)-L(x)

\displaystyle=\psi(x)-\psi_{0}(x)+\psi_{0}(x)-L(x)=\chi(x)(\phi(x)-\psi_{0}(x))+\psi_{0}(x)-L(x).

To see that (
4.66
) holds,
note first of all that from the definition of
\chi
we
have

\displaystyle\nabla\chi(x)

\displaystyle=\phantom{{}+{}}f_{1}^{\prime}(x^{1})\chi_{0}(x+(\rho,0))\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}+f_{1}(x^{1})\nabla\chi_{0}(x+(\rho,0))

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+f_{2}^{\prime}(x^{1})\eta(x))\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}+f_{2}(x^{1})\nabla\eta(x)

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+f_{3}^{\prime}(x^{1})\chi_{0}(x-(\rho,0))\vec{\varepsilon}_{1}+f_{3}(x^{1})\nabla\chi_{0}(x-(\rho,0)),

while from the definition of the functions
f,f_{1}
and
f_{3}

we have for every
x^{1}\in[-\rho,\rho]
that

\displaystyle f_{1}(x^{1})f(x_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv 0,

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime}(x^{1})f(x_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv 0,

\displaystyle f_{1}(x^{1})(1-f(x_{1}))

\displaystyle\equiv f_{1}(x^{1}),

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\prime}(x^{1})(1-f(x_{1}))

\displaystyle\equiv f_{1}^{\prime}(x^{1}),

\displaystyle f_{3}(x^{1})f(x_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv f_{3}(x^{1}),

\displaystyle f_{3}^{\prime}(x^{1})f(x_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv f_{3}^{\prime}(x^{1}),

\displaystyle f_{3}(x^{1})(1-f(x_{1}))

\displaystyle\equiv 0,

\displaystyle f_{3}^{\prime}(x^{1})(1-f(x_{1}))

\displaystyle\equiv 0,

consequently from (
4.61
)
we deduce the estimate

\displaystyle|\nabla\chi(x)(\phi(x)-\psi_{0}(x))|\leq C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}

\displaystyle\bigg(|f_{1}^{\prime}(x^{1})|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+f_{1}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+|f_{2}^{\prime}(x^{1})|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+f_{2}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+|f_{2}^{\prime}(x^{1})|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+f_{2}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}+|f_{3}^{\prime}(x^{1})|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{1+\alpha}+f_{3}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\alpha}\bigg),

which then implies

\displaystyle\|\nabla\chi(\phi-\psi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}\leq C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}{(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)}})}.

From estimate (
4.62
) we immediately deduce that

\displaystyle\|\chi(\nabla\phi-\nabla\psi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\leq C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}{(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)}})},

consequently using (
4.54
) we can estimate

\displaystyle\|\nabla\psi-\nabla L\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla\chi(\phi-\psi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}+\|\chi(\nabla\phi-\nabla\psi_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}+\|\psi_{0}-L\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}+C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta))

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

which proves (
4.66
).
To establish (
4.67
), the argument is similar:
from the properties of
\chi
one deduces the estimates

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\chi(x)(\phi(x)-\psi_{0}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\bigg(f_{1}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-1+\alpha}+f_{3}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-1+\alpha}\bigg),

\displaystyle|\nabla\chi(x)(\nabla\phi(x)-\nabla\psi_{0}(x))|

\displaystyle\leq C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}\bigg(f_{1}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}+\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-1+\alpha}+f_{3}(x^{1})\left|\begin{pmatrix}x^{1}-\rho\\
x^{2}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{-1+\alpha}\bigg),

and, since
\alpha>0
, the right-hand sides
of these last two inequalities are in
L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))
.
Consequently also thanks to (
4.54
)
and (
4.63
)
we estimate

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\psi\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}\chi(\phi-\psi_{0})\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}+2\|\nabla\chi(\nabla\phi-\nabla\psi_{0})\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}+\|\chi(\nabla^{2}\phi-\nabla^{2}\psi_{0})\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}+C[\nabla\psi_{0}]_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}(0)})}+C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta))

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rho}(0))}+C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)),

which then implies, thanks to (
4.53
),
the estimate (
4.67
).
The rest of the proof now follows the same lines as in
the proof of lemma
4.4
.
∎

4.3
Morrey-Type Estimates and Conclusion

For a conformal map
\Phi:B_{1}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

which is a minimiser for the total curvature energy in the class

\mathcal{F}_{B_{1}(0)}(\Gamma,\vec{n}_{0},A)

as that in theorem
1.2
,
there are two possibilities.

The first one is that
\Phi
is a minimal surface, that is

\displaystyle D\Area(\Phi)w=0\quad\text{for all }w\in C^{\infty}_{c}({B_{1}(0)},\mathbb{R}^{m}),

and this implies that
\Phi
satisfies

\displaystyle\Delta\Phi^{i}=0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({B_{1}(0)})\quad\text{for }i=1,\ldots m.

The regularity up to the boundary
for the first case is classic, and is essentially the one
for the Plateau problem,
for which we refer to
[
24
,
23
]
.

We now study the second possibility.

Lemma 4.8

(Interior Morrey-type Estimates)
.

Let
\Phi:B_{1}(0)\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

be a conformal Lipschitz immersion with

L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form
which is an interior minimiser for the total curvature energy in

\mathcal{F}_{B_{1}(0)}
at a fixed area value, that is

\displaystyle E(\Phi)\leq E(\Psi)

for every map
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}_{B_{1}(0)}

coinciding with
\Phi
outside some compact subset of
B_{1}(0)

and so that
\Area(\Psi)=\Area(\Phi)
.
Assume that
\Phi
is not a minimal surface and set

\displaystyle\zeta=\|D\Area(\Phi)\|>0.

(4.68)

Then, there exists some
r_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{r^{-\gamma}\int_{B_{r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}:p\in\overline{B_{1/2}(0)},\,0<r<r_{0}\bigg\}\leq C_{0},

for constants
\gamma>0
and
C_{0}>0
depending only on

\zeta
and
\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}
.

Proof of lemma
4.8
.

In what follows, we denote by
C
a positive constant
(possibly varying line to line) which is independent of
\Phi
,
and with
C_{0}
a positive constant
which depend on
\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}

and
\zeta
.

Step 1: constructing a suitable competitor.

Since
\Phi
is not a minimal surface,
we may choose some non-zero

w\in C^{\infty}_{c}({B_{1}(0)},\mathbb{R}^{m})

such that
D\mbox{Area}(\Phi)w>\zeta/2
.
We let
\delta,\varepsilon_{0}>0
to be as in
lemma
4.4

and whose size is specified in what follows.
Let
r_{0}>0
be sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{\int_{B_{4r_{0}}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}:p\in\overline{B_{1/2}(0)}\bigg\}<\varepsilon_{0}.

We now fix arbitrary
p\in\overline{B_{1/2}(0)}

and
0<r<r_{0}
and
(similarly as done as in the proof of lemma
4.4
)
for
\varepsilon_{0}
sufficently small as in lemma
4.1
,
we have that

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{4r}(p)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3r}(p))}\leq C_{0},

(4.69)

where
(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{4r}(p)}
denotes the average of
\lambda_{\Phi}
over
B_{4r}(p)
.
If
\varepsilon_{0}
is sufficiently small as in lemma
4.3
,
then there exists a conformal affine immersion

L
whose conformal factor we denote by
\mathrm{e}^{\nu}
,
such that the estimates

\displaystyle\|\Phi-L\|_{W^{2,2}(B_{r}(p))}

\displaystyle<\delta\|\nabla\Phi\|_{L^{2}({B_{2r}(p)})},

(4.70)

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(p))}

\displaystyle<\delta,

(4.71)

are satisfied.

By combining (
4.69
)-(
4.71
),
we deduce

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3r}(p))}

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{4r}(p)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3r}(p))}+|(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{4r}(p)}-\nu|

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{4r}(p)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3r}(p))}+\|\lambda_{\Phi}-(\lambda_{\Phi})_{B_{4r}(p)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(p))}+\|\lambda_{\Phi}-\nu\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r}(p))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+\delta,

consequently we pointwise estimate from above and below

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}(1-\delta-o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(x)}\leq C_{0}(1+\delta+o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{\nu}\quad\text{for }x\in B_{3r}(p).

(4.72)

Hence we consider

\displaystyle\Psi=\begin{cases}\psi&\text{in }B_{\rho}(p),\\
\Phi&\text{in }{B_{1}(0)}\setminus B_{\rho}(p).\end{cases}

where
\rho\in[r/2,r]
and
\psi
are given as in lemma

4.4
.

Thanks to (
4.28
),
we have that, if
\delta
is chosen sufficiently small, there holds

\displaystyle|D\Area(\Psi)w-D\Area(\Phi)w|=|D\Area(\psi)w-D\Area((\Phi|_{B_{\rho}(p)})w|<\frac{\zeta}{4},

and consequently,

\displaystyle D\Area(\Psi)w>\frac{\zeta}{4}>0.

We consider the function given by

\displaystyle a(t)=\Area(\Psi+tw),\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

Let
\varepsilon>0
be sufficiently small so that,
for every
t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]
,
\Psi+tw

defines a Lipschitz immersion (with
L^{2}
-bounded
second fundamental form).
Then
a
is continuously differentiable in
[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]

with

\displaystyle a^{\prime}(t)=D\Area(\Psi+tw)w

\displaystyle=-2\int_{B_{1}(0)}\langle\vec{H}_{\Psi+tw},w\rangle,\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Psi+tw},\quad\text{for }t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon],

and in particular

\displaystyle a^{\prime}(0)

\displaystyle>\frac{\zeta}{4}>0.

By the inverse function theorem, we deduce that, after
possibly shrinking
\varepsilon
,

a
defines a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism
of
[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]
onto

[\Area(\Psi)-\varepsilon,\Area(\Psi)+\varepsilon]

and

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta}{8}\leq a^{\prime}(t)\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}\quad\text{for }t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon].

(4.73)

Thanks to (
4.28
),
we have that, if
\delta
is chosen sufficiently small, there holds

\displaystyle|\Area(\Psi)-\Area(\Phi)|=|\Area(\psi)-\Area((\Phi|_{B_{\rho}(p)})|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

so we may find a unique

\overline{t}\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]

so that

\displaystyle\Area(\Psi+\overline{t}w)=\Area(\Phi).

We then set

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi}=\Psi(x)+\overline{t}w(x)\quad\text{for $x$ in }B_{1}(0).

Then
\overline{\Psi}
is a Lipschitz immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form,
and by construction there holds
\Area(\Psi)=\Area(\Phi)
.

Step 2: comparison of
\Phi
with
\overline{\Psi}
.

By the minimality of
\Phi
we then have

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\leq\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\vec{\II}_{\overline{\Psi}}|^{2}_{g_{\overline{\Psi}}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\overline{\Psi}},

(4.74)

Following a computation analogous to (
[
49
, Lemma A.5]
),
the term on the right-hand-side can be expanded to

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\vec{\II}_{\overline{\Psi}}|^{2}_{g_{\overline{\Psi}}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\overline{\Psi}}=E(\Psi+\overline{t}w)=E(\Psi)+\overline{t}DE(\Psi)w+R^{\Psi}_{w}(\overline{t}),

where
R^{\Psi}_{w}(\overline{t})
is a remainder term satisfying

\displaystyle|R^{\Psi}_{w}(\overline{t})|\leq C_{\Psi,w}\overline{t}^{2},

and,
since
\Phi
is a minimiser for the total curvature energy
with prescribed area,
we may write (we use the divergence form of the Willmore
equation, valid for weak immersions, introduced in
[
57
]
), there holds
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle DE(\Psi)w

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}DW(\Psi)w

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{1}(0)}\langle\nabla\vec{H}_{\Psi}-3\pi_{\vec{n}_{\Psi}}(\nabla\vec{H}_{\Psi})+\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{\Psi}\wedge\vec{H}_{\Psi}),\nabla w\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{1}(0)\setminus B_{\rho}(p)}\langle\nabla\vec{H}_{\Phi}-3\pi_{\vec{n}_{\Phi}}(\nabla\vec{H}_{\Phi})+\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{\Phi}\wedge\vec{H}_{\Phi}),\nabla w\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\phantom{{}=}\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{\rho}(p)}\langle\nabla\vec{H}_{\psi}-3\pi_{\vec{n}_{\psi}}(\nabla\vec{H}_{\psi})+\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{\psi}\wedge\vec{H}_{\psi}),\nabla w\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=c\int_{B_{1}(0)\setminus B_{\rho}(p)}\langle\nabla\Phi,\nabla w\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\phantom{{}=}\frac{1}{4}\int_{B_{\rho}(p)}\langle\nabla\vec{H}_{\psi}-3\pi_{\vec{n}_{\psi}}(\nabla\vec{H}_{\psi})+\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{\psi}\wedge\vec{H}_{\psi}),\nabla w\rangle\,\mathrm{d}x,

so that we can simply estimate:

|DE(\Psi)w|\leq C_{\Phi,w}
.

By the mean value theorem and the estimates
(
4.27
)
and (
4.72
) it holds

\displaystyle|\overline{t}|

\displaystyle=|a^{-1}(a(\overline{t}))-a^{-1}(a(0))|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in J}|(a^{-1})^{\prime}(\xi)||a(\overline{t})-a(0)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{\zeta}|\Area(\Psi+tw)-\Area(\Psi)|

\displaystyle\leq C|\Area(\Phi)-\Area(\Psi)|

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\rho}(0)})

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\mathrm{e}^{2\nu},

It follows that

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\vec{\II}_{\overline{\Psi}}|^{2}_{g_{\overline{\Psi}}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\overline{\Psi}}\leq\int_{B_{1}(0)}|\vec{\II}_{\Psi}|^{2}_{g_{\Psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Psi}+C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

Thanks to (
4.26
),
the above estimate and (
4.74
) then imply

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho(p)}}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\leq C_{0}\int_{B_{r}(p)\setminus B_{r/2}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}(\delta+o(\delta)).

(4.75)

Step 3: monotonicity of Area.

Thanks to (
4.72
),
for every

0<s<r
we can estimate

\displaystyle\Area(\Phi|_{B_{s}(p)})=\int_{B_{s}(p)}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=2\int_{B_{s}(p)}\mathrm{e}^{2\lambda_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}s^{2}\leq C_{0}\mathrm{e}^{4\nu}\frac{s^{2}}{r^{2}}\int_{B_{r}(p)}|\nabla\Phi|^{2}\mathrm{d}x.

(4.76)

Step 4: obtaining the Morrey decrease.

For any
0<\eta<1/2
,
thanks to (
1.11
) and lemma

4.1
,
there exists
C>0
independent of
p
and
r

so that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\eta r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}+C\varepsilon_{0}\right)\int_{B_{r}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\int_{B_{\eta r}(p)}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+\Area(\Phi|_{B_{\eta r}(p)}),

and so by estimate (
4.75
) we
deduce that there holds

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\eta r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{2}+C\varepsilon_{0}\right)\int_{B_{r}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+C_{0}\int_{B_{2\eta r}(p)\setminus B_{\eta r}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+C_{0}\left((\delta+o(\delta))+\eta^{2}r^{2}\right)\mathrm{e}^{2\nu}.

By using (
4.72
) and (
4.76
)
and adding

C_{0}\int_{B_{\eta r}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}
to both hand-sides
and dividing by
1+C_{0}
yields

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\eta r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\eta^{2}/2+C\varepsilon_{0}+C_{0}}{C_{0}+1}\right)\int_{B_{r}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\left(\frac{C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta)+\eta^{2})}{C_{0}+1}\right)\Area(\Phi|_{B_{r}(p)}).

If
\eta
and
\delta
are chosen sufficiently small so that

\displaystyle\beta:=\max\left\{\frac{\eta^{2}/2+C\varepsilon_{0}+C_{0}}{C_{0}+1},\frac{C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta)+\eta^{2})}{C_{0}+1}\right\}<1,

we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\eta r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\leq\beta\int_{B_{r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

for any
p\in\overline{B_{1/2}(0)}

and any
0<r<r_{0}

where
0<\beta<1
does not depend on
r
or
p
.
This inequality can be now iterated and interpolated
to yield

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\leq r^{\log_{1/\eta}(1/\beta)}\frac{1}{r_{0}^{\log_{1/\eta}(1/\beta)}}\int_{B_{r_{0}}(p)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

for any
r<\eta r_{0}
,
where
\beta
and
\eta
depend only on
\zeta
and

\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}
.
After relabelling
r_{0}
, and setting
\gamma:=\log_{1/\eta}(1/\beta)
we can concludes
of the proof of the lemma.
∎

For boundary points, we have the following.
Recall that we write
\partial B_{1}^{+}(0)=I+S
,
where
I
is the base diameter and
S
is the upper semi-circle,
and when we say that a Lipschitz immersion “has geometric boundary data of class
C^{1,1}
”
if its boundary curve and boundary Gauss map are
C^{1,1}
up to re-parametrization
and not in a point-wise sense, see definition
1.5
–(ii).

Lemma 4.9

(Boundary Morrey-type Estimates)
.

Let
\Phi:B_{1}(0)^{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

be a conformal Lipschitz immersion with

L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form so that

\displaystyle E(\Phi)\leq E(\Psi)

for every map
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}
that coincides with
\Phi
outside
some subset
K
of
\overline{B_{1}^{+}(0)}
with
\dist(K,S)>0

and having the same geometric boundary data of
\Psi
in
I
, that is

\displaystyle\Psi(I)=\Phi(I)\quad\text{and}\quad\vec{n}_{\Psi}(I)=\vec{n}_{\Phi}(I),

and the same area, that is
\Area(\Phi)=\Area(\Phi)
.
Assume that the boundary geometric data of
\Phi
on
I
are of class
C^{1,1}

and that
\Phi
is not a minimal surface.
Let
\zeta>0
be as in (
4.68
).
Then,
there exist some
0<\overline{r}<r_{0}<1

so that

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{r^{-\gamma}\int_{B_{r}^{+}(p)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}:p\in r_{0}I,\,0<r<\overline{r}\bigg\}<+\infty,

for some constant
\gamma>0
.

Remark 4.10
.

Two elementary facts that will be used in the proof of lemma

4.9
are the following:

(i)

If
\vec{e}=(\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2})
denotes the
coordinate frame of the map
\Phi
, then we have

\displaystyle\vec{e}(x)=(\mathbf{t},\star(\mathbf{t}\wedge\vec{n}_{0})(\sigma(x^{1})),\quad x^{1}\simeq(x^{1},0)\in I,

where
\mathbf{t}
denotes the tangent vector of the boundary curve
and
\sigma_{\Phi}
is some homeomorphism with
\sigma^{\prime}(x^{1})=\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(x^{1},0)}
.
In particular, since the boundary data are assumed of class
C^{1,1}
, we see that
for
i=1,2
we can estimate, for every
1<p<\infty
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\tau}\vec{e}_{i}\|_{L^{p}(I)}\leq C_{0}\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\cdot,0)}\|_{L^{p}(I)},

where
C_{0}
depends only on the geometric boundary data.
Furthermore, for every
1<p<\infty
,
if we set
\Phi_{r}(x)=\Phi(rx)
,
x\in B_{1}^{+}(0)

one can compute that

\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi_{r}}(\cdot,0)}\|_{L^{p}(I)}=r^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\cdot,0)}\|_{L^{p}(rI)}

and thus deduce that the
L^{p}
-norm of
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}(\cdot,0)}

is
decreasing
with respect to rescaling in the domain, for
0<r<1
.

(ii)

For a generic immersion of an open domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
,

X:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
and a diffeomorphism

f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
, denoting for brevity
denoting

\vartheta=O(\|\nabla f-\mathbbm{1}_{m\times m}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})})
and

\eta=O(\|\nabla^{2}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})})
,
we can deduce the point-wise estimates

\displaystyle\left(1-\vartheta\right)g_{X}

\displaystyle\leq g_{f\circ X}\leq(1+\vartheta)g_{X},

\displaystyle(1-\vartheta)\mathrm{d}vol_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{d}vol_{{f\circ X}}\leq(1+\vartheta)\mathrm{d}vol_{X},

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}(f\circ X)|_{g_{f\circ X}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\vartheta)(\eta+\vartheta|\nabla^{2}X|^{2}_{g_{X}})\leq C_{0}(1+|\nabla^{2}X|^{2}_{g_{X}}),

where
C_{0}
is a constant depending only on
f
.

Proof of Lemma
4.9
.

In what follows, we denote by
C
a positive constant
(possibly varying line to line) which is independent of
\Phi
,
and with
C_{0}
a positive constant
dependeding only on
\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{(2,\infty)}(B_{1}(0))}
,
and on the geometric boundary data at
I
of
\Phi
.

Step 1: preliminaries and reductions.

We fix
p>1
and a suitably small
\varepsilon_{0}
that will be specified below.
Since the boundary data of
\Phi
along
I

are of class
C^{1,1}
, we may find some
0<r_{0}<1

and a
C^{1,1}
-homeomorphism
f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

(that is,
f
and its inverse belong to
C^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{m},\mathbb{R}^{m})
),
so that
(f\circ\Phi)|_{B_{r_{0}}^{+}(0)}
has flat boundary data along
r_{0}I

as in the sense of definition
4.6
.
Up to further reducing
r_{0}
, we also assume that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r_{0}}^{+}(0)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}}\|_{L^{p}(r_{0}I)}<\varepsilon_{0}.

(4.77)

Note that, since
the Lagrangian
\Phi\mapsto\int(1+|\nabla^{2}\Phi^{2}|_{g_{\Phi}}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

is invariant with respect to re-parametrizations
and the conformal factor is decreasing with respect
to rescalings (see remark
4.10
–(i)),
(
4.77
) implies in particular that,
having set for brevity
\Phi_{r_{0}}(x)=\Phi(r_{0}x)
,
there holds

\displaystyle\sup\bigg\{\int_{B_{4r}^{+}(x)}(|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{r_{0}}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi_{r_{0}}}}+1)\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi_{r_{0}}}+\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi_{r_{0}}}}\|_{L^{p}(x+rI)}:x\in\frac{1}{2}I,\;0<r\leq\frac{1}{16}\bigg\}<\varepsilon_{0}.

We will work from now on, omitting the subscript,
with
\Phi_{r_{0}}(x)=\Phi(r_{0}x)
in place of
\Phi
.
Also, since
\Phi
is not a minimal surface, there exists some

w\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B_{1}^{+}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})
so that

\displaystyle D\Area(\Phi)w\geq\zeta/2>0.

(4.78)

Finally, thanks to lemma
1.6
there exist some bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism

\phi:B_{1}^{+}(0)\to B_{1}^{+}(0)
so
that
f\circ\Phi\circ\phi
is conformal
moreover up to further composing with a conformal
self-map of
B_{1}^{+}(0)
we may suppose that
\phi(\pm 1)=\pm 1
and
\phi(0)=0
,
hence that the geometric boundary data on
I
are sent (globally) onto themselves:
f(\Phi(I))=f(\Phi(\phi(I)))
.

Step 2
.
For simplicity of notation we will prove the Morrey-type decay at

x=0
; the one for other points in
(1/2)I
is analogous.
Since
\phi
is bi-Lipschitz, we may find a sufficiently big
N\in\mathbb{N}

and a sufficiently big
M=M(N)
so that, for every
0<r<1
, we have

\displaystyle B_{r/2^{M}}^{+}(0)\subset\phi(B_{r/{2^{N}}}(0))\subset B_{r}^{+}(0).

(4.79)

Let
0<r\leq 1/16
be fixed.
For a sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{0}
, thanks to lemma

4.7
we may find a

\rho\in[r/2^{N+1},r/2^{N}]

and an immersion

\psi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{B_{\rho}^{+}(0)},\mathbb{R}^{m})

which satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \psi&=f\circ\Phi\circ\phi&&\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0)\cap B_{1}^{+}(0),\\
\nabla\psi&=\nabla(f\circ\Phi\circ\phi)&&\text{on }\partial B_{\rho}(0)\cap B_{1}^{+}(0),\end{aligned}

has flat boundary data on
\rho I

and satisfies the estimates
(
4.43
),
(
4.44
) and
(
4.45
)
with
r/2^{N}
in place of
r
and
f\circ\Phi\circ\phi
in place of
\Phi
,
and in particular

\displaystyle\int_{B^{+}_{r/2^{N+1}}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}_{g_{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}\leq C_{0}\int_{B^{+}_{r/2^{N}}(0)\setminus B_{r/2^{N+1}}(0)}|\nabla^{2}(f\circ\Phi\circ\phi)|^{2}\mathrm{d}vol_{f\circ\Phi\circ\phi}+C_{0}\int_{B^{+}_{r/2^{N}}(0)}\mathrm{d}vol_{f\circ\Phi\circ\phi}

(4.80)

Hence we set

\displaystyle\Psi=\begin{cases}f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi^{-1}&\text{in }\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0)),\\
\Phi&\text{in }B_{1}^{+}(0)\setminus\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))\end{cases}

From (
4.44
) and
(
4.45
)
we deduce

\displaystyle|\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))})-\Area(f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi^{-1})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))}),

\displaystyle\|D\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))})-D\Area(f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi^{-1})\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B^{+}_{r/2^{N}}(0))}),

where
C_{0}>0
depends on
\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{2\infty}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}

and on
f
.
Thanks to (
4.78
),
we have that, if
\delta
(and accordingly
\varepsilon_{0}
)
is chosen sufficiently small, there holds

\displaystyle|D\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))})w-D\Area(f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi^{-1})w|<\frac{\zeta}{4},

and consequently,

D\Area(\Psi)w>\zeta/4.

We then consider the function given by

\displaystyle a(t)=\Area(\Psi+tw),\quad t\in\mathbb{R}.

Let
\varepsilon>0
be sufficiently small so that,
for every
t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]
,
\Psi+tw

defines a Lipschitz immersion (with
L^{2}
-bounded
second fundamental form).
Then
a
is continuously differentiable in
[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]

with

\displaystyle a^{\prime}(t)=D\Area(\Psi+tw)w

\displaystyle=-2\int_{B_{1}^{+}(0)}\langle\vec{H}_{\Psi+tw},w\rangle,\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Psi+tw},\quad\text{for }t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon],

and in particular

a^{\prime}(0)>\zeta/4>0.

By the inverse function theorem, we deduce that, after
possibly shrinking
\varepsilon
,

a
defines a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism
of
[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]
onto

[\Area(\Psi)-\varepsilon,\Area(\Psi)+\varepsilon]

and

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta}{8}\leq a^{\prime}(t)\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}\quad\text{for }t\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon].

(4.81)

By choosing
\delta
sufficiently small we may suppose that

\displaystyle|\Area(\Phi)-\Area(\Psi)|=|\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(p))})-\Area(f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi^{-1})|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

so we may find a unique

\overline{t}\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon]

so that

\Area(\Psi+\overline{t}w)=\Area(\Phi).

We then set

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi}=\Psi(x)+\overline{t}w(x)\quad\text{for $x$ in }B_{1}^{+}(0).

Then
\overline{\Psi}
is a Lipschitz immersion with
L^{2}
-bounded second fundamental form,
and by construction there holds
\Area(\overline{\Psi})=\Area(\Phi)
.
Similarly as done in the proof of lemma
4.8
,
following a computation analogous to (
[
49
, Lemma A.5]
),
the total curvature energy of
\overline{\Psi}
can be expanded with respect to
\overline{t}

as

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}^{+}(0)}}|\vec{\II}_{\overline{\Psi}}|^{2}_{g_{\overline{\Psi}}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\overline{\Psi}}=E(\Psi+\overline{t}w)=E(\Psi)+\overline{t}DE(\Psi)w+R^{\Psi}_{w}(\overline{t}),

with
|DE(\Psi)w|\leq C_{\Phi,w}
and

R^{\Psi}_{w}(\overline{t})
satisfies

|R^{\Psi}_{w}(\overline{t})|\leq C_{\Psi,w}\overline{t}^{2}.

By the mean value theorem,
we have the estimate

\displaystyle|\overline{t}|

\displaystyle=|a^{-1}(a(\overline{t}))-a^{-1}(a(0))|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in J}|(a^{-1})^{\prime}(\xi)||a(\overline{t})-a(0)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{\zeta}|\Area(\overline{\Psi})-\Area(\Psi)|

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{\zeta}|\Area(\Phi)-\Area(\Psi)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))}),

where
C_{0}
depends on

\|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}\|_{L^{2\infty}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}
, on
f

and on
\zeta
and
this yields the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{{B_{1}(0)}}|\vec{\II}_{\overline{\Psi}}|^{2}_{g_{\overline{\Psi}}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\overline{\Psi}}\leq\int_{B_{1}(0)}|\vec{\II}_{\Psi}|^{2}_{g_{\Psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Psi}+C_{0}(\delta+o(\delta))\Area(\Phi|_{\phi(B_{\rho}^{+}(0))}).

We write

\displaystyle\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}=\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}(0))}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+4\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

We have, on the one hand, thanks to lemma
4.2

and the choice of
N
,
the estimate

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{B_{r/2}^{+}(0)}|\nabla\lambda_{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.82)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8}\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}\bigg(\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}}\|_{L^{p}(rI)}\bigg),

where
C_{0}
depends only on
\zeta
and
\vec{n}_{0}
;
on the other hand, the comparison of
\Phi
with
\overline{\Psi}

yields (we use the pointwise a.e. estimates in
remark
4.10
–(ii))

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\vec{\II}_{f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi}|^{2}_{g_{f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{{f^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\phi}}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0)}|\vec{\II}_{f^{-1}\circ\psi}|^{2}_{g_{f^{-1}\circ\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{f^{-1}\circ\psi}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}(f^{-1}\circ\psi)|^{2}_{g_{f^{-1}\circ\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{f^{-1}\circ\psi}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0)}(1+|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}_{g_{\psi}})\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}

(by (
4.80
))

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0)}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+C_{0}\int_{B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0)\setminus B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}(f\circ\Psi\circ\phi)|^{2}_{g_{f\circ\Psi\circ\phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\psi}+C_{0}\int_{B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0)}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{f\circ\Psi\circ\phi}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0))}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0)\setminus B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi},

and so all in all,

\displaystyle\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\vec{\II}_{\Phi}|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\leq C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0))}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0)\setminus B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

(4.83)

By combining (
4.82
) and (
4.83
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0))}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N}}^{+}(0)\setminus B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}\bigg(\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}}\|_{L^{p}(rI)}\bigg)

and so adding

C_{0}\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

to both hand-sides

\displaystyle\int_{\phi(B_{r/2^{N+1}}^{+}(0))}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{1/2+C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}+C_{0}}{C_{0}+1}\bigg)\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}

\displaystyle\phantom{{}\leq{}}+C_{0}\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}}\|_{L^{p}(rI)}.

Choosing
\varepsilon_{0}
sufficiently small so that

C_{0}\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1/4
, with (
4.79
)
and the above inequality
we deduce that, for every
0<r<1/4
there holds

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r/2^{M+1}}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\leq\beta\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}+C_{0}\|\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{1}^{+}(0))}r,

where
0<\beta<1
depends only on
C_{0}
.
As in the proof of lemma
4.8
,
this equality can now be iterated and interpolated to yield
the existence of some
\gamma>0
so that

\displaystyle\sup_{r<r<\overline{r}}\bigg\{r^{-\gamma}\int_{B_{r}^{+}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\Phi|^{2}_{g_{\Phi}}\,\mathrm{d}vol_{\Phi}\bigg\}<+\infty,

for some suitably small
\overline{r}<1/16
.
After going back to the original scale,
this yields to the conclusion of the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem

1.2
(conclusion)
and of Theorem

1.3
.

Let
\Phi
be a conformal map which is a minimiser for the total curvature energy in

\mathcal{F}_{B_{1}(0)}(\Gamma,\vec{n}_{0},A)
,
and let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{N}
be its branch points
(recall that none of them lays on the boundary).
For every sufficiently small
\delta>0
, the conformal factor
of
\Phi
,
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\Phi}}=|\nabla\Phi|^{2}/\sqrt{2}

is then uniformly bounded from above and below
in
\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{N}B_{\delta}(a_{i})
,
and consequently, covering
\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{N}B_{\delta}(a_{i})

with finitely many balls, thanks to
lemma
4.8

and lemma

4.9
,
we deduce that its Hessian
\nabla^{2}\Phi
belongs to the Morrey space

L^{2,a}(B_{1}(0)\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{N}B_{\delta}(a_{i}))

for some
a>0
(see e.g.
[
33
,
34
]
),
and consequently, by Morrey’s Dirichlet growth theorem (
[
51
]
, see also

[
34
, Theorem 5.7]
) that

\Phi\in C^{1,a/2}(\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{N}B_{\delta}(a_{i}))
.

Similarly as done in
[
57
]
,
\Phi

satisfies the Euler-Lagrange equation for the Poisson problem, i.e.
the Willmore equation in divergence form
plus a Lagrange multiplier term for the area constraint,
in the sense of distributions away from the branch points.
Being however
\vec{H}_{\Phi}=\mathrm{e}^{-2\lambda_{\Phi}}\Delta\Phi/2

in
L^{2}(B_{1}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m})
, the contributions at the branch points can only consist on
sum of Dirac masses and thus we deduce that

\displaystyle\divop\left(\nabla\vec{H}_{\Phi}-3\pi_{\vec{n}_{\Phi}}(\nabla\vec{H}_{\Phi})+\star(\nabla^{\perp}\vec{n}_{\Phi}\wedge\vec{H}_{\Phi})+c\nabla\Phi\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\vec{\alpha}_{i}\delta_{a_{i}},\quad\text{in }\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(B_{1}(0),\mathbb{R}^{m}),

for some
\alpha_{N},\ldots\alpha_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}

and
c\in\mathbb{R}
.
Since however
\Phi
is a variational immersion,
i.e. it is obtained as a critical point of the total curvature energy,
a simple implicit function theorem argument similar to that
used in the proof of lemmas

4.8

and

4.9

allows to construct suitable comparisons for
\Phi

thanks to which one deduces that

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha}_{i}=0\quad\text{for every\quad}\quad i=1,\ldots,N.

(We refer the reader to
[
54
]
for the details of the above statements).
This yields that
\Phi
